# Earlier 2026 Chase Tissue ranks

The historical comparison covers **1,154 races, 8,637 ranked starters and 237 distinct race dates** from 2026-01-01 through 2026-10-14. All available ranks, T1–T34, are included regardless of saved-filter membership.

## A. Dataset and provenance

- The previous comparison of Chase V2 T3 and “Chases - 2026 non summer” used the intersection of the pinned 1,220-race Chase promotion reference and the isolated 2026 Jump cache through 14 October. There were 1,154 matching race IDs. Exactly 66 reference races lay outside the cache overlap (2026-10-16 through 2026-10-29). Every joined race passed date, race subtype, exact starter-roster and winner-ID agreement; no joined race failed full-result proof. The isolated reader verifies provider race and ride IDs, runner and horse identity, canonical outcome classification, and the independent final starter count. The October reference and November prospective captures are separate.
- This replay uses the stored frozen Shadow probabilities directly. The frozen Chase V2 loader and generation manifest agree, the model embeds the identical Chase Shadow V1 base parameters and checksum, and every adjustment weight is zero. The live Chase comparator and the earlier comparison both sort descending by probability and break exact ties by runner UUID. There were 0 exact probability-tie races. Maximum absolute deviation of the probability sum from one was 4.44e-16.
- The replay consumes only frozen probability and runner ID to assign rank. Final SP, settlement and the saved rule are joined or checked separately. The original reconciliation records prior-only comment features and used a historical starter-field reconstruction. The saved books contain probabilities and runner IDs with outcome labels, and lack original timestamped per-runner scoring vectors. The target outcome and SP are absent from the scoring interface. The historical feature code can use actual field size and the promotion reference requires an eventual complete starter roster. Pre-off field composition and feature publication timing therefore cannot be certified from this evidence. See the [promotion reconciliation](tissue-generation-2026-11.md#chase-promotion-reconciliation).
- All T3 figures reconciled to the earlier comparison’s JSON, including **1,145 selections = 1,093 T3-only + 52 shared** with the saved rule, 187 winners, 622 covered market selections and −£176.758 at final SP. The split is provenance evidence only and does not gate any rank in this report.
- Canonical v3 determines resolved wins/losses and uncapped £1 final-SP settlement. Non-runners were outside the frozen starter rosters (859 additional cached non-runner rows across 528 races); the replay never promotes a replacement. The included fields contain zero void races, zero unresolved ranked starters and 0 dead heats. Winner counts and strike use resolved win flags. A/E uses fractional dead-heat credit and returns use the canonical divisor, which divides gross decimal SP including the stake.
- Final SP is a **retrospective market benchmark**. Winning and losing results can count as settled independently of price. There are 0 missing valid SPs among ranked selections. Market E, A/E and average market rank use normalized inverse SP on the same selections from 626 complete books. The existing helper withheld 528 books, including 528 failing the cached actual-runner-count check (528 with the cached count including proved non-runners) and 0 with at least one missing valid starter SP. The replay preserves the earlier benchmark's count guard even when independent starter proof is available. Average market rank uses competition ranks for equal SP. Exact-size Top-k comparisons use shorter SP first and runner UUID as the tiebreaker.
- The frozen model files, promotion reference, generation manifest, prior diagnostic, ten isolated source pins and 1,154 local full-result payloads used in the join all matched their read-time hashes on recheck. The analysis used local files only. The relevant test run passed 90 tests across nine files.

## E. Tissue versus final-SP market

Both rankings start with the same 626 complete starter books on 214 dates. At each cutoff both select the same number of runners per race, capped at its field size. Capture is the proportion of races with a winner in the selected set. A/E compares the selected winner credit with the sum of those runners’ normalized SP probabilities. The final column gives the number of market books where the fixed-slot cutoff splits equal SPs.

### Sensitivity using all independently verified starter fields

All 1,154 races have valid SPs for every proved starter. For this check only, the final-SP normalization uses each verified starter roster directly, including the 528 races withheld by the original cached count guard. The normalizer and ranking tie rules are identical. Normalized probabilities agree to within 1e−12 on the original 626 books. Sections B–H otherwise retain the original market benchmark.

## H. Largest-winner sensitivity

The removal calculations drop the full race and its stake for each rank, in descending order of winning gross. Gross shares show the largest and two largest winning races as shares of all winning gross. The A/E interval is the existing seeded 2,000-replicate race-date cluster percentile bootstrap. These are separate, exploratory intervals and do not adjust for comparison across ranks and cuts. With zero benchmark winners, a 0–0 bootstrap is degenerate and gives no useful upper bound. Sparse tails have unstable intervals.

## I. Conclusions

- **T3 was close to market expectation.** Across all 1,145 selections, 187 won against 180.11 model-expected winners. The canonical market subset had 106 wins against 106.15 expected, A/E **0.999** (95% date-cluster interval 0.83–1.18). Final-SP P/L was **−£176.76 (−15.44%)**, falling to −£218.76 (−19.14%) without the two biggest winning races. On the matched T1–T5 races, T3’s market A/E was 1.045, behind T2 at 1.096, and all five ranks lost money.
- **T7 is the clearest exploratory follow-up among ranks with hundreds of races.** Its 50 winners in 588 races gave market A/E **1.201** in the 297 benchmark races (95% interval 0.82–1.61). The proof-based market sensitivity gave **1.170** (0.91–1.45). T7 lost £26.88 at final SP, falling to −£92.88 after removing two winners. Its positive 5–<11 band contained 27 winners from 176 stakes, while 21+ contributed only +£6 across 206 stakes. The result was therefore broader than a single long-priced winner, but varied from −32.32% ROI in Q1 to +60.00% in the 25-race partial Q4 sample. Handicap and non-handicap results also diverged. These cuts describe the observed variation and provide no basis for choosing a new subset.
- **T9 illustrates the effect of market coverage.** It had A/E 1.319 on the original 152 benchmark selections (95% interval 0.66–2.10), but 1.034 across its 340 verified starter books. It lost £142.46 at final SP (−41.90%). Among T1–T5, T1 led overall by market A/E (1.054) and T2 led on the matched fields. Both were loss-making and the overall A/E intervals include 1.
- **The largest headline figures came from sparse ranks.** T18 had the highest canonical market A/E (**9.877**, one win in six benchmark races against about 0.10 expected), the best £ P/L (**+£46**) and the best ROI (**+219.05%**). The sole winner was Martator at decimal SP 67, whose removal leaves 20 losses. T17 also depended on a sole winner, at SP 41, and T16 turned from +£8 to −£10 when its biggest winner was removed. T7 was closest to break-even among ranks with at least 100 stakes. T6 had the worst cash P/L (**−£252.92**). T15 and T20–T34 tied for worst ROI (**−100%**). The lowest measurable canonical A/E was **0.000**, shared by T13–T15, T17 and T19–T23. T24–T34 had no canonical market benchmark.
- **The final-SP market captured more winners at every tested cutoff.** On the canonical 626 books, its lead ranged from 5.59 to 14.06 percentage points. All four paired date-cluster intervals favour the market, and adding the other 528 verified starter books preserves that ordering. The near-one A/E values for the top sets are consistent with their different market expectations and capture rates.
- **No individual rank demonstrates a robust market or profitability advantage.** Winner concentration, incomplete original market coverage, short windows, historical field reconstruction and multiple exploratory comparisons limit the inference. All three ranks with positive final-SP P/L lost money when their largest winner was removed.
